### User

_source line 367 · 2026-05-16T06:09:38.310Z_

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am an independent scientific reviewer (Reviewer role) for the project `struct_self_consist_ie`, which studies confidence signals for test-time scaling in information extraction. My job is to independently verify experimental conclusions, review Director action plans, and post findings via `reviewer_post_to_director`. The session covers reviewing Director's Action Matrices for Rounds 11 and 12 of paper review (borderline 3.25/5, DDL 2026-05-25), auditing provenance data, verifying worker outputs, and answering technical questions about the codebase. All output must be in Chinese.

   Key review requests received:
   - Round 11 Action Matrix review: MF1 (RE benchmark), MF2 (ε circularity), MF3 (contribution reframe), SF1-7, DIV1-4
   - ROADMAP updates review after D104 (TACRED added), D105 (TACRED cancelled), D106 (entity-token LP dispatched)
   - Worker output reviews: provenance audit, VC quartile analysis, Bertoldi citation check, entity-token LP experiment (failed then succeeded)
   - Round 12 Action Matrix review: MF1 (by-construction reframe), MF2 (title change to NER), SF2 (entity-token LP), SF8 (gold-filtering error)
   - Three specific technical questions from Director about token-level logprobs, title choice, and gold-filtering

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Correlation-selection gap**: Confidence signals predict quality (ρ up to 0.45, AUROC 0.75) but fail at best-of-N selection (Δ≈0 vs greedy despite 13-18pp oracle headroom)
   - **LP score compression**: 60% of instances have within-sample LP range < 0.05 (ε=0.05 threshold); median LP range = 0.041 nats
   - **Five confidence signals**: SJ (Soft Jaccard), FK (Fleiss' Kappa), EM (Exact Match), VC (Voting Confidence), LP (Log-Probability)
   - **Instance-level vs per-sample signals**: 4/5 signals are instance-level aggregates; LP is the only per-sample signal
   - **MBR (Minimum Bayes Risk)**: Converts instance-level signals to per-sample rankings via consensus-proximity utility, but utility correlates with F1 across but not within instances
   - **Free-form ablation**: Removing constrained decoding does NOT recover LP variance (std ratio 0.97) → compression is model/task intrinsic
   - **Entity-token LP**: Isolating entity-mention tokens shows higher variance (4× on SciERC) but WORSE selection (-1.68pp SciERC, -4.06pp CoNLL)
   - **Gold-filtering**: Pipeline excludes instances with empty gold annotations (CoNLL 3453→2756, SciERC 551→529)
   - **Conditional filtering**: Further excludes greedy_f1=0 instances; two different filters exist (correct: greedy_f1>0; incorrect: any sample F1>0)
   - **VC quartile stratification**: Non-monotonic relationship between VC and LP selection effectiveness on NER
   - **Scale ablation**: 2×2 matrix {CoNLL, SciERC} × {Qwen3-8B, Qwen3-4B} + LLaMA 3.2-3B cross-family
   - **Z-score normalization**: Monotonic transform within instances → does not change within-instance ranking or selection F1 (no-op for best-of-N)

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`/root/autodl-tmp/struct_self_consist_ie/code/data_utils.py`**
     - Contains dataset loaders: `load_scierc`, `load_conll2003`, `load_wnut17`, `load_ace05` (NotImplementedError stub at line 199)
     - No `load_tacred` function exists — confirmed zero TACRED support
     - Important for assessing MF1 TACRED feasibility

   - **`/root/autodl-tmp/struct_self_consist_ie/code/sampling.py`**
     - Modified by entity-token LP worker: line 218 now saves `token_logprobs` field
     - Line 185: `sp_kwargs["logprobs"] = 1` — requests per-token logprobs from vLLM
     - Lines 198-203: Originally only saved `cumulative_logprob` and `mean_logprob`
     - Line 623: `inst_dict["logprobs"] = [lp["mean_logprob"] for lp in stoch_logprobs[idx]]`
     - Critical for understanding that token-level logprobs were NOT previously saved

   - **`/root/autodl-tmp/struct_self_consist_ie/code/run_mvp_pilot.py`**
     - Line 237: `nonempty_mask = [len(inst["gold"].get("entities", [])) > 0 for inst in sampled]` — gold-empty filtering
     - Lines 243-245: All correlations computed on filtered data
     - Line 253: `cond_mask = [nonempty_mask[i] and instance_f1s[i] > 0 ...]` — conditional filter
     - Line 67: `--collect_logprobs` argument
     - Important for SF8 gold-filtering verification

   - **`/root/autodl-tmp/struct_self_consist_ie/code/entity_token_analysis.py`** (234 lines)
     - New file created by worker for entity-token LP analysis
     - Compares entity-only LP vs full-sequence LP variance and selection F1

   - **`/root/autodl-tmp/struct_self_consist_ie/output/exp_entity_token_lp/results.json`**
     - Key results:
       - SciERC: entity LP median variance 0.000644 (4× full), selection -1.68pp (worse), entity fraction 5.3%
       - CoNLL: entity LP median variance 2.02e-10 (zero), selection -4.06pp (catastrophic), entity fraction 7.7%
       - Z-score is no-op for selection

   - **`/root/autodl-tmp/struct_self_consist_ie/output/vc_quartile_analysis.json`**
     - SciERC NER: Q1 LP+1.55pp, Q2 -0.65pp, Q3 +1.97pp, Q4 -0.41pp (non-monotonic)
     - SciERC RE: Q4 LP +4.01pp (clearest positive signal)
     - CoNLL: Q3/Q4 degenerate (VC=1.0), Q1 all negative

   - **`artifacts/provenance_audit_c032_c023_c021.md`**
     - c032: Paper ρ=0.011 correct (exp_012_rerun_1024, correct filter n=238); yaml 0.002 stale; registry 0.1457 wrong filter
     - c023: Paper Q1=0.110/Q4=0.473 correct (exp_009_v2_re_rerun gold-filtered); registry outdated pre-gold-filter
     - c021: Paper ρ=0.089 correct (exp003_wnut17_eval); yaml 0.062 untraceable

   - **`artifacts/bertoldi_citation_check/result.md`**
     - Bertoldi et al. (2024) is hallucinated. Closest real paper: Chang et al. (2024) AIME cancer staging (arXiv:2404.13149)

   - **`paper_provenance.yaml`** (version 5, 2026-05-15)
     - Still NOT updated despite audit confirming stale entries — c032/c023/c021 entries unchanged

   - **`ROADMAP.md`** (last updated D106, 2026-05-16 05:40 UTC)
     - Current stage: Round 12 handling, DDL 2026-05-25 (~8 days)
     - Critical path: entity-token LP re-inference → Paper Agent writing → Round 13 → submit
     - 4× RTX 5090 idle

   - **`decisions.yaml`** — 105 decisions (D001-D105 visible, D106 in ROADMAP)
     - D003: ACE05 dropped (LDC license)
     - D019/D062: TACRED infeasible
     - D095: ε=0.05 robust (sweep plateau)
     - D104: TACRED added (user authorized) — overridden by D105
     - D105: TACRED cancelled, title/scope + provenance audit + VC quartile
     - D106: Round 12 Action Matrix — entity-token LP + MBR reframe + title NER

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **Registry too large (170K chars)**: Output saved to file, used subagent to parse with jq/python
   - **decisions.yaml too large (83K chars)**: Same approach — subagent parsing
   - **SSH pipe character blocked**: `grep -r "tacred|TACRED"` rejected due to `|` metacharacter. Fixed by running separate grep commands for each pattern
   - **compute_coverage_matrix failed**: "cannot use 'list' as a dict key (unhashable type: 'list')" — skipped, not critical
   - **experiment_plan.yaml not in allowed file list**: read_project_file rejected it, only specific files allowed
   - **Entity-token LP worker first attempt failed**: Worker died before producing any output (likely context window exhaustion). No server-side changes made. Recommended split into code-mod + inference steps. Second attempt succeeded.

5. Problem Solving:
   - **TACRED feasibility**: Conclusively ruled out through code inspection (zero support), data inspection (not on server), and structural analysis (sentence-level RE vs set extraction incompatibility). Director adopted my recommendation in D105.
   - **Provenance discrepancies**: Three alarming differences (c032 72× gap) turned out to be stale yaml/registry entries — actual paper values are correct. Traced each to specific source files.
   - **Token-level logprobs availability**: Confirmed pipeline only saved aggregated mean LP. Identified exact code locations in sampling.py. Worker successfully modified code and re-ran inference.
   - **Gold-filtering truth**: Confirmed via code that ALL selection computations use gold-filtered data (nonempty_mask). Paper's "full set" claim is factually wrong.
   - **Z-score normalization**: Worker correctly identified it's a no-op for best-of-N selection (monotonic transform preserves rank).
   - **Entity-token LP hypothesis**: Partially confirmed (entity tokens have more variance) but remedy backfires (selection worse) — strengthens "fundamental limitation" narrative.

6. All User Messages:
   - "[System 触发审查]: Round 11 审稿报告 — 请审核 Director Action Matrix" — Initial Round 11 review request with MF1-MF3, SF1-SF7, DIV1-4, and 3 decision questions
   - "Continue from where you left off." — (multiple times, no action needed)
   - "[系统通知 — roadmap_updated]: TACRED RE + VC-filtered LP + 14项写作修复" — ROADMAP updated with TACRED (D104)
   - "[系统通知 — roadmap_updated]: D105: 取消 TACRED, 改 title/scope + provenance audit + VC quartile" — D105 adopted my recommendations
   - "[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_analysis_provenance_audit_c032_c023_c021_20260516T045025]" — Provenance audit worker done
   - "[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_analysis_vc_quartile_d105_20260516T045336]" — VC quartile worker done
   - "[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_analysis_provenance_audit_d105_20260516T045309]" — Second provenance audit worker done
   - "[System 触发审查]: Round 12 审稿报告 + Director Action Matrix — 请独立审核" — Round 12 review with MF1-2, SF1-8, Q1-Q6, 3 technical questions
   - "[系统通知 — roadmap_updated]: D106: Round 12 Action Matrix dispatched" — D106 update
   - "[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_research_bertoldi_citation_check]" — Bertoldi citation check done
   - "[服务器重启后首条消息] + [系统通知 — worker_done:worker_code_exp_entity_token_lp (status=failed)]" — Server restart + entity-token LP failed
   - "[系统通知 — worker_done:worker_code_exp_entity_token_lp (status=done)]" — Entity-token LP second attempt succeeded

7. Pending Tasks:
   - **Review entity-token LP results and post to Director**: Results are in `results.json` but I haven't posted my review yet. This is the most immediate task.
   - **Provenance yaml still not updated**: c032/c023/c021 entries remain stale (flagged twice)
   - **Registry cleanup**: exp_tacred_re_benchmark (PREPARING/killed), exp_entity_token_lp (still ALLOCATED, should be DONE)
   - **Report.md cleanup**: Still lists cancelled experiments (TACRED, VC LP cascade)

8. Current Work:
   I was in the process of reviewing the entity-token LP experiment results when the summary was requested. I had:
   - Confirmed re-inference completed successfully for both SciERC and CoNLL
   - Read the full `results.json` with detailed metrics
   - Verified `sampling.py` was modified (line 218: `token_logprobs` field)
   - Verified `samples.jsonl` now contains per-token logprobs and token texts
   - Confirmed GPU is idle (experiment finished)
   - Registry still shows ALLOCATED (not updated to DONE)
   
   The results show:
   - Entity-token LP has higher variance (SciERC 4×) but WORSE selection (-1.68pp SciERC, -4.06pp CoNLL)
   - Z-score normalization is a no-op for selection
   - CoNLL entity-token LP is catastrophically compressed (median variance ~0)
   - Entity tokens comprise only 5-8% of total tokens
   
   I need to post my review of these results to the Director via `reviewer_post_to_director`.

9. Optional Next Step:
   Post review of entity-token LP experiment results to Director. The worker notification said "请审查 Agent 的处理结果" (please review the Agent's results). Key points to cover:
   - Hypothesis (d) format-token dilution is partially confirmed (entity tokens have more variance)
   - But the remedy backfires — entity-token LP selection is WORSE
   - This STRENGTHENS the "fundamental limitation" narrative rather than providing a remedy
   - CoNLL entity-token LP is essentially zero-variance (median 2e-10)

   - Paper framing recommendation: present as evidence that LP compression is not merely dilution

(2024) \"Beyond Self-Consistency: Ensemble Reasoning in LLMs\"\n\n## 结论：**不存在。Hallucinated reference。**\n\n## 证据\n\n搜索了 Google、arXiv、Semantic Scholar、Springer，均未找到任何 \"Bertoldi\" 为作者的相关论文。\n\n存在一篇标题相近的真实论文，但作者、完整标题和主题均不同：\n\n| | 审稿推荐的引用 | 实际存在的论文 |\n|---|---|---|\n| **作者** | Bertoldi et al. | Chang, C.H., Lucas, M.M., Lee, Y., Yang, C.C., Lu-Yao, G. |\n| **标题** | Beyond Self-Consistency: Ensemble Reasoning in LLMs | Beyond Self-Consistency: Ensemble Reasoning Boosts Consistency and Accuracy of LLMs in **Cancer Staging** |\n| **主题** | 通用 LLM 推理 (implied) | 医疗 NLP（癌症分期） |\n| **Venue** | 未知 | AIME 2024 (Artificial Intelligence in Medicine) |\n| **arXiv** | 无 | [2404.13149](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.13149) |\n\n审稿推荐的引用很可能是对 Chang et al. (2024) 的错误记忆/幻觉——作者名完全错误，标题被截断且去掉了领域限定词 \"Cancer Staging\"。\n\n## 建议\n\n不应引用 \"Bertoldi et al. (2024)\"。如需引用相关工作，可考虑真实论文：\n\n> Chang, C.H., Lucas, M.M., Lee, Y., Yang, C.C., & Lu-Yao, G. (2024). Beyond Self-Consistency: Ensemble Reasoning Boosts Consistency and Accuracy of LLMs in Cancer Staging. *AIME 2024*. arXiv:2404.13149\n\n但注意该论文聚焦医疗领域癌症分期，与本项目（结构化自一致性 IE）的相关性需评估。\n",

        "content": "# Provenance Audit: c032 / c023 / c021\n\n**Date**: 2026-05-16\n**Auditor**: automated provenance trace\n**Scope**: Three claims in paper_provenance.yaml where registered values diverge from paper text\n\n---\n\n## c032: logprob conditional RE ρ\n\n- **Provenance yaml claim**: paper says ρ=0.002\n- **Registry value**: exp_015_metrics_expansion → re.logprob_rho_cond = 0.1457\n- **Current paper text**: ρ=0.011 (experiments.tex:50, appendix.tex:137)\n\n### Source trace\n\nThe **current paper** says ρ=0.011 in two places:\n1. `experiments.tex:50` — \"LP †RE collapses to ρ=0.011\"\n2. `appendix.tex:137` — heatmap caption: \"LP RE collapses to near-zero (ρ=0.011)\"\n\nThe value **0.01119** is in:\n- `output/exp_012_rerun_1024/report.json:100` — `re_logprob_baseline.conditional.rho = 0.011185364740047766`\n- `output/exp_012_rerun_1024/all_signals_5signal.json:403` — same value\n- Conditions: SciERC RE, N=8, T=1.0, max_tokens=1024, **correct conditional filter** (greedy_f1 > 0, n=238)\n\n### Why the discrepancy\n\n| Value | Source | Filter | n_cond | Status |\n|-------|--------|--------|--------|--------|\n| 0.002 | provenance yaml claim | unknown | — | **STALE** — paper was updated to 0.011; 0.002 untraceable in any output file |\n| 0.1457 | exp_015_metrics_expansion | WRONG: any sample F1 > 0 | 331 | Wrong filter inflates n, changes correlation |\n| **0.011** | exp_012_rerun_1024 | CORRECT: greedy_f1 > 0 | 238 | Current paper value |\n\nThe `conditional_filter_diagnosis.json` documents that exp_015 (and several other scripts) used the wrong conditional filter (\"any sample F1 > 0\" instead of \"greedy_f1 > 0\"), inflating the conditional set from n=238 to n=331 for RE. This is the root cause of the registry's 0.1457.\n\n### Conclusion\n\n**Provenance yaml is stale.** The paper was already corrected from 0.002 to 0.011. The current paper value (0.011 from exp_012_rerun_1024, correct conditional filter) is correct. The registry value (0.1457) uses a known-wrong conditional filter and should be annotated as deprecated.\n\n---\n\n## c023: RE SJ ρ by sentence length (Q1/Q4)\n\n- **Paper values**: Q1=0.110, Q4=0.473, Fisher z p=0.004\n- **Registry values**: exp_009_capability_stratification → Q1=0.447, Q4=0.540\n\n### Source trace\n\nPaper values match **exp_009_v2_re_rerun** exactly:\n\nFrom `output/exp_009_v2_re_rerun/summary.txt`:\n```\nStratification by Sentence Length QUARTILES (primary, independent)\n  Q1 (low)   n=117  SJ 0.110[-0.08,0.29]\n  Q2         n=93   SJ 0.308[0.09,0.50]\n  Q3         n=92   SJ 0.372[0.18,0.54]\n  Q4 (high)  n=95   SJ 0.473[0.28,0.62]\n\nFisher z-test low_vs_high:\n  sj   z=-2.878  p=0.004*\n```\n\nAll three paper values (Q1=0.110, Q4=0.473, p=0.004) match the v2 rerun output character-for-character.\n\nFrom `output/exp_009_v2_re_rerun/results.json`:\n- `by_sent_len_quartiles.\"Q1 (low)\".signals.sj.rho = 0.10961735` → rounds to 0.110\n- `by_sent_len_quartiles.\"Q4 (high)\".signals.sj.rho = 0.47332...` → rounds to 0.473\n- Fisher z low_vs_high sj p = 0.00435... → rounds to 0.004\n\n### Why the registry differs\n\nThe registry values (Q1=0.447, Q4=0.540) come from the **original exp_009_capability_stratification**, which:\n1. Did NOT apply gold-empty filtering (included 154 instances with empty gold RE annotations)\n2. Used all 551 raw instances instead of the filtered 397\n\nThe v2 rerun applied proper gold-filtering (551 → 397 after removing gold-empty), which is the mandated pipeline for all ρ values in the paper.\n\n| Value set | Source | Gold filter | n_RE | Status |\n|-----------|--------|-------------|------|--------|\n| Q1=0.447, Q4=0.540 | exp_009 original | No | 551 | **Outdated** — pre-gold-filter |\n| **Q1=0.110, Q4=0.473** | exp_009_v2_re_rerun | Yes | 397 | Current paper value |\n\n### Conclusion\n\n**Paper values are correct.** They come from exp_009_v2_re_rerun with proper gold-filtering. The registry entry for exp_009_capability_stratification is outdated and should be annotated as superseded by exp_009_v2_re_rerun.\n\n---\n\n## c021: WNUT SJ ρ\n\n- **Provenance yaml claim**: paper says ρ=0.062 (p=0.106)\n- **Registry values**: exp_003_ner_wnut17 → 0.082; exp_003_wnut17_eval → 0.089\n\n### Source trace — current paper\n\nThe **current paper** (appendix.tex:222, Table tab:wnut) shows:\n```\nSJ  .089  .020  .517  .288  <1e-7\n```\ni.e., SJ full-set ρ = **0.089**, p = 0.020\n\nThis matches `output/exp003_wnut17_eval/report.json`:\n- `correlation_softjaccard_vs_f1_full.rho = 0.08883161` → rounds to 0.089\n- `correlation_softjaccard_vs_f1_full.p_value = 0.01970` → rounds to 0.020\n- n = 689 (after gold-empty filtering)\n\n### Tracing the 0.062\n\nThe value ρ=0.062 appears ONLY in the **old deprecated paper** at:\n- `artifacts/_project/paper/experiments.tex:181` — \"SJ achieves ρ=0.062 (p=0.106, not statistically significant)\"\n\nThis file is in `artifacts/_project/paper/` which is excluded from compilation per constraint C001. The current compiled paper at `docs/paper/sections/` does NOT contain 0.062 for WNUT.\n\nSearching all output files for 0.062 in WNUT context yields **no matches**. The closest values found are:\n- exp003_wnut17_pilot: SJ full rho = 0.0825 (different seed)\n- exp003_wnut17_eval: SJ full rho = 0.0888\n- exp_009_v2 global SJ rho (SciERC RE, not WNUT): 0.0625\n\nThe value 0.062 for WNUT SJ is **untraceable** — it does not appear in any analysis output file. It may have originated from:\n1. An earlier experiment run that was overwritten\n2. A different seed/N configuration\n3. A manual transcription error in the old draft\n\n### Conclusion\n\n**Provenance yaml is stale.** The current paper correctly uses ρ=0.089 from exp003_wnut17_eval (N=16, T=1.0, gold-filtered n=689). The 0.062 value from the old paper draft is untraceable and no longer appears in the compiled paper.\n\n---\n\n## Summary\n\n| Claim | Provenance yaml | Current paper | Ground truth source | Status |\n|-------|----------------|---------------|-------------------|--------|\n| c032 LP cond RE ρ | 0.002 | **0.011** | exp_012_rerun_1024 (correct filter, n=238) | Provenance stale; paper correct |\n| c023 RE SJ Q1/Q4 | Q1=0.447/Q4=0.540 (registry) | **Q1=0.110/Q4=0.473** | exp_009_v2_re_rerun (gold-filtered) | Registry outdated; paper correct |\n| c021 WNUT SJ ρ | 0.062 | **0.089** | exp003_wnut17_eval (n=689) | Provenance stale; paper correct |\n\n**All three paper values are correct in the current compiled paper.** The discrepancies exist only between the provenance yaml / registry entries and the paper — not between the paper and the ground truth data. The provenance yaml and registry entries need updating.\n",

      }

```text
## Summary

I've retrieved the full contents of the results.json file and the related report.md from the directory `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_1/projects/struct_self_consist_ie/artifacts/exp_entity_token_lp/`.

### Results.json Contents

The file contains comparative performance metrics across two datasets (SciERC and CoNLL2003) comparing:
- **Greedy F1**: baseline selection method
- **Full-LP (full sequence logprob) selection F1**: mean logprob across all output tokens
- **Entity-LP (entity-token logprob) selection F1**: mean logprob of tokens corresponding to entity mentions
- **Majority vote F1**: ensemble baseline
- **Oracle F1**: upper bound

Key metrics include variance/range statistics, tied fractions, entity token statistics, and disagreement analysis between methods.

### Related Files

**report.md** exists in the same directory and provides a comprehensive summary:
- **Setup**: Details on model (Qwen3-8B), sampling parameters, and methodology
- **Key Finding**: Entity-LP selection is strictly worse than full-LP selection on both datasets (2.03pp loss on SciERC, 3.30pp on CoNLL)
- **Root Cause Analysis**: On CoNLL, 79.4% of instances have entity-LP range < 0.01, meaning the model is too confident on entity tokens to distinguish sample quality. On SciERC, despite 4x higher variance, the small entity-token fraction (5.3%) makes the signal noisier.

The conclusion is that full-sequence LP remains a better proxy than entity-token LP for sample quality, though both methods are limited as indicators of actual output correctness.
```

</details>

{
  "text": "## 审查报告 — exp_entity_token_lp（entity-token LP 实验）\n\n**结论**: PASS（实验设计合理、结论可信，附两条 info）\n\n### 核实结果\n\n**1. 实验结论核实**\n\n假设 (d) \"格式 token 稀释 LP 信号\"的验证结果：\n\n| 指标 | SciERC | CoNLL |\n|------|--------|-------|\n| Entity-LP 方差 vs Full-LP | 4.0× (median) | ~0 (median 2e-10) |\n| Entity-LP selection ΔF1 | **-1.68pp** | **-4.06pp** |\n| Full-LP selection ΔF1 | +0.35pp | -0.76pp |\n| Entity-LP tied fraction | 35.0% | **79.4%** |\n\n结论核实：\n- ✅ SciERC 上 entity tokens 确实方差更高（4×），证实格式 token 有稀释效应\n- ✅ 但 entity-LP 选择反而更差（-2.03pp vs full-LP），说明**更高方差 ≠ 更好信号**\n- ✅ CoNLL entity-LP 几乎零方差（median 2e-10），79.4% 实例 tied → 模型对 NER entity tokens 过度自信，完全无区分力\n- ✅ Z-score 正确识别为 no-op（单调变换不改变 instance 内排名）\n\n**2. 对论文 framing 的意义**\n\n这个结果**强化** \"fundamental limitation\" 叙事：\n- LP 压缩不是格式 token 稀释的 artifact——即使只看 entity tokens，信号仍然无效或更差\n- 特别是 CoNLL：entity tokens 的 LP 方差反而比 full-sequence **更低**，说明模型对关键 token 最自信、最无区分力\n- 这为 \"LP 作为选择信号的根本局限性\" 提供了更强的证据支撑\n\n**3. 数据完整性**\n\n- SciERC: n_valid=529（551 - gold-empty = 529，与已知 gold-filtering 一致 ✅）\n- CoNLL: n_valid=2756（3453 - gold-empty = 2756，一致 ✅）\n- disagreement 分析合理：不一致时 full-LP wins > entity-LP wins（SciERC 105:80, CoNLL 242:94）\n\n### 发现\n\n- [severity: info] 实验设计和结论均可信。Entity-token LP 假设被部分验证（方差更高）但 remedy 失败（选择更差），这是一个有价值的 negative result，直接支撑论文核心论点。\n\n- [severity: info] Registry 状态滞后：exp_entity_token_lp 在 registry 中仍为 ALLOCATED，应更新为 DONE 并补全 conclusion 字段。\n\n### 论文使用建议\n\n建议在 paper 中将此结果框架为：\"We further test whether LP compression stems from format-token dilution by isolating entity-mention tokens. Entity-token LP shows 4× higher variance on SciERC but *worse* selection (-2.0pp), and near-zero variance on CoNLL (79% tied). This rules out dilution as the primary cause and points to an intrinsic model confidence pattern.\"\n\n### 遗留事项提醒\n\n1. **paper_provenance.yaml 仍未更新**：c032/c023/c021 三条 stale 条目已审计确认两次，但 yaml 至今未修正。这不影响论文正确性（paper text 是对的），但 provenance tracking 失效增加后续出错风险。\n2. **Registry 清理**：exp_tacred_re_benchmark（PREPARING/killed）应标 CANCELLED；exp_entity_token_lp 应标 DONE。",
  "project": "struct_self_consist_ie"
}

## Round 13 Action Matrix 独立审核请求

Round 13 审稿仍 borderline（R1: 3.5, R2: 3.0）。以下是我的逐条分析和初步方案，请独立审核可行性、遗漏、技术风险。

DDL: 2026-05-25（~8天）。autodl-5090 4×RTX 5090 32GB 全部空闲。

---

### MF1 — Contribution framing 仍混淆 definitional/empirical（11轮）

**诊断**：Paper Agent 在 Round 12 删除了所有 "by construction" 文字并重写了 Contribution 1/2，但 reviewers 仍认为 Abstract line 4-5、Intro C1、§4.3 line 85 将 definitional 与 empirical 混为一谈。删词不够——需要结构性重组。

**方案**：
1. §3.1 将 "instance-level signals assign identical scores" 明确标记为 **methodological premise**（定义性前提），不作为 finding 呈现。一句话交代后不再反复渲染。
2. Contribution 1 重组为两个 **纯 empirical findings**：
   - (a) MBR consensus-proximity 将 instance-level 信号转化为 per-sample rankings（Table 2 证明被执行了），但 selection 仍失败——因为 utility 与 F1 在 across-instances 维度相关但 within-instance 不相关
   - (b) LP 作为唯一 natively per-sample 信号被压缩至近乎无差异（median 0.041 nats），entity-token overconfidence 是根因
3. Abstract 相应调整：不再说 "4/5 signals can't select"（definitional），改为说 MBR 产生的 per-sample rankings 无 within-instance discriminative power + LP compressed
4. **需要新数据**：Q1 要求 MBR within-instance utility-F1 ρ 具体数值——这是支撑 reframe 的关键空白。需要 Main Agent 从现有数据计算。

**风险**：这是第 11 次尝试修这个问题。如果仅靠措辞调整仍然被打回，可能需要考虑更根本的结构变化（如将整个 instance-level signal 分析移入 background/related work）。

---

### MF2 — Effective test bed 仅 SciERC（14轮，新演化）

**诊断**：Title 已改为 NER，但 reviewers 指出 effective evidence 仅 SciERC（n=529）。CoNLL 是 ceiling control，WNUT 是 collapse，这两个不支撑 selection gap 本身。

**两个选项**：
- **(a) 收窄 claims**：明确 gap 在 "medium-difficulty regime" 验证，CoNLL/WNUT 是 boundary condition analysis 而非 gap 的 direct evidence。纯写作修改。
- **(b) 加 Few-NERD 或 OntoNotes subset**：通用领域、中等难度 NER benchmark。需要 fine-tune + N=16 sampling + full signal pipeline。预估 ~8h GPU（fine-tune 4h + inference 3h + analysis 1h）。

**我的倾向**：先走 (a) 收窄 claims，同时启动 (b) Few-NERD 实验。(b) 如果结果 positive 则整合；如果 negative 也可作为 boundary condition 加入。(a) 是保底，(b) 是加分。

**请审核**：
1. Few-NERD 作为第二 test bed 是否合适？（entity type diversity、difficulty level、社区认可度）
2. 8h GPU 预估是否合理？
3. 如果 Few-NERD 结果不显示 gap，对论文影响是什么？

---

### SF1 — ρ=0.45 headline 来自 ceiling dataset

**方案**：将 headline 改为 SciERC primary test bed 数值（VC ρ=0.379），或改为 "ρ up to 0.45 (CoNLL, ceiling control) / 0.38 (SciERC, primary test bed)"。
**Assigned**: Paper Agent

---

### SF2 — 纯诊断无有效 remedy

**方案**：在现有数据上做 LP×VC combination 分析（零 GPU）：
- Strategy: if VC > median → use LP to rank within instance; else → random
- 比较 combination selection F1 vs LP-only vs VC-only
- 即使 negative 也提供信息量

**请审核**：这个 combination strategy 是否合理？有没有更好的 lightweight remedy 可以尝试？

---

### SF3 — RE 覆盖策略不统一

**方案**：将 RE 结果全部移到 Appendix 作为 "Exploratory Extension"。理由：单 benchmark、40% zero-F1、†RE 标记 >15 处导致 hedging noise。Free-form RE reversal 在 appendix 仍可被引用但不占主文空间。

**请审核**：移 appendix 是否是正确策略？还是应该保留在主文但重新定位？

---

### SF4 — 缺少 greedy (argmax) baseline（新 issue）

**方案**：用现有 fine-tuned 模型跑 T=0 argmax inference（SciERC + CoNLL）。一次 forward pass，~1h GPU。
- 如果 greedy > all N=16 selection → gap 更 striking（"can't beat the free baseline"）
- 如果 selection > greedy → 有 some value，量化 improvement margin

**请审核**：
1. 这个实验是否值得做？（vs 只在 limitation 中承认）
2. 技术上是否能用 vLLM T=0 直接出 argmax？还是需要修改 sampling pipeline？

---

### SF5 — Yao et al. 增量贡献

**方案**：重心从 SJ structural matching → MBR failure mechanism + LP diagnostic chain。SJ 降为 methodological choice。
**Assigned**: Paper Agent

---

### Minor Items

| ID | 方案 | 分配 |
|----|------|------|
| M1 Kendall W | Round 12 已弱化，检查是否足够 | Paper Agent |
| M2 Abstract compressed | 精简 abstract，减少 mechanical details | Paper Agent |
| M3 LP hypotheses 编号 | Round 12 已修正 "four mechanisms"，但 reviewer 仍标记——需核实实际文本 | Paper Agent |
| M4 Bib issues | 修正 mejer2011→2010, feng2025, 检查 4 unmatched | Paper Agent |
| M5 Fisher p 术语 | 统一 "Fisher combined p" | Paper Agent |
| M6 SJ naming | 已降为 preference，footnote 已有 | 不动 |

---

### Questions 回应

| Q | 回应方案 |
|---|---------|
| Q1 MBR within-instance utility-F1 ρ | **必须计算**。从现有 SCS 数据提取 per-instance utility-F1 correlation。零 GPU。关键数据空白。 |
| Q2 LP×VC combination | = SF2，零 GPU 分析 |
| Q3 Oracle-free proxy for LP | 分析现有数据中是否有 feature 可预测 LP discriminative power。如无 → 在 Limitations 承认 |
| Q4 Entity-LP CoNLL vs SciERC | 在论文中解释：CoNLL entity type diversity 低 (4 types) + 模型对常见 entity 更自信 → 更极端压缩 |
| Q5 4B diversity data | 检查是否已有 4B 的 output diversity 指标（unique outputs per instance）。如无则计算 |

---

### 战略问题：是否冲 Main Conference？

R1=3.5（borderline conference），R2=3.0（Findings）。升到 Main Conference 需要：
1. MF1 definitively resolved（这次必须成功）
2. MF2 addressed（claims 收窄 + 可能加 Few-NERD）
3. SF2 addressed（至少一个 pilot remedy 结果）
4. SF4 greedy baseline（新数据）

如果 MF1 在第 12 次尝试仍失败，或 Few-NERD 结果 negative，可能需要接受 Findings track。**但当前策略仍是冲 Main Conference**——8 天时间充足。

请给出你的独立评估。

### Tool result

_source line 470 · 2026-05-16T06:58:45.398Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Pn7e1gKr6GjLncMg4GncWr`</summary>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	Self-consistency yields large gains on discrete-answer tasks, but named entity recognition (NER) produces \textit{sets} of entities with partial overlap, where no majority vote analog exists.
3	We evaluate five confidence signals across three NER benchmarks, with one RE benchmark as an exploratory extension, at two model scales (4B and 8B).
4	All signals achieve moderate-to-strong quality estimation (Spearman $\rho$ up to 0.45), yet none improves best-of-$N$ selection despite substantial oracle headroom---the \textit{correlation-selection gap}.
5	Four instance-level signals assign identical scores to all candidates; when converted to consensus-proximity rankings (MBR), they produce per-sample scores but do not improve selection.
6	Log-probability---the only natively per-sample signal---is too compressed within instances to discriminate (median within-instance range 0.041 nats).
7	A 4-seed free-form decoding ablation confirms NER compression is not an artifact of constrained decoding; on RE, LP selection reverses direction under free-form decoding ($+1.8{\pm}0.1$~pp), suggesting regime-dependent behavior.
8	Consensus-proximity selection (MBR) correlates with F1 across instances but not within them.
9	Signal rankings shift with task difficulty and collapse at the capability boundary; closing the gap likely requires learned per-sample scoring.
10	\end{abstract}
11	
```

      "content": "\\begin{abstract}\nSelf-consistency yields large gains on discrete-answer tasks, but named entity recognition (NER) produces \\textit{sets} of entities with partial overlap, where no majority vote analog exists.\nWe evaluate five confidence signals across three NER benchmarks, with one RE benchmark as an exploratory extension, at two model scales (4B and 8B).\nAll signals achieve moderate-to-strong quality estimation (Spearman $\\rho$ up to 0.45), yet none improves best-of-$N$ selection despite substantial oracle headroom---the \\textit{correlation-selection gap}.\nFour instance-level signals assign identical scores to all candidates; when converted to consensus-proximity rankings (MBR), they produce per-sample scores but do not improve selection.\nLog-probability---the only natively per-sample signal---is too compressed within instances to discriminate (median within-instance range 0.041 nats).\nA 4-seed free-form decoding ablation confirms NER compression is not an artifact of constrained decoding; on RE, LP selection reverses direction under free-form decoding ($+1.8{\\pm}0.1$~pp), suggesting regime-dependent behavior.\nConsensus-proximity selection (MBR) correlates with F1 across instances but not within them.\nSignal rankings shift with task difficulty and collapse at the capability boundary; closing the gap likely requires learned per-sample scoring.\n\\end{abstract}\n",
      "numLines": 11,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 11
    }
  },

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01YaDSQ2gcVfJ8Xrvk6gGdzv`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Self-consistency---majority voting over multiple samples---yields large gains on discrete-answer reasoning \citep{wang2023selfconsistency}, and test-time compute scaling has emerged as a complementary axis to model scaling \cite{snell2025scaling, brown2024monkeys}.
4	But named entity recognition (NER) poses a structurally different challenge: the output is a \textit{set} of entities, two samples may extract overlapping but non-identical sets, and there is no obvious majority to vote on.
5	
6	Prior work on confidence estimation for LLM-based IE has conflated \textit{quality estimation} (which inputs does the model handle reliably?) with \textit{sample selection} (which of $N$ outputs is best?).
7	Existing self-consistency methods \cite{wang2023selfconsistency, chen2024usc, taubenfeld2025cisc, kang2025selfcertainty} assume outputs can be grouped by equivalence---an assumption that breaks for entity sets with partial overlap.
8	\citet{yao2026consistency} examine consistency--accuracy correlations for LLM-based IE using exact-match agreement; we extend their work by introducing structural matching (Soft Jaccard, a variant using per-type max cardinality; \S\ref{sec:signals}) that captures partial overlap, separating quality estimation from best-of-$N$ selection, and evaluating across a three-dataset difficulty gradient with a free-form decoding ablation.
9	
10	Our central finding is the \textbf{correlation-selection gap} (Figure~\ref{fig:overview}): consistency signals achieve moderate-to-strong quality estimation ($\rho$ up to 0.45, substantially exceeding random baselines) but fail at sample selection despite substantial oracle headroom.
11	The gap has two causes: instance-level signals (4 of 5) assign identical QE scores to all candidates; consensus-proximity (MBR) converts these to per-sample rankings, but utility correlates with F1 across instances, not within them (\S\ref{sec:discussion}).
12	LP---the only natively per-sample signal---is too compressed within instances to discriminate (median range 0.041 nats; \S\ref{sec:discussion}).
13	
14	\begin{figure*}[t]
15	\centering
16	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig_overview}
17	\caption{The correlation-selection gap. \textbf{Left}: high inter-sample agreement correlates with high F1 (quality estimation). \textbf{Right}: instance-level signals assign identical QE scores; MBR converts these to per-sample rankings but does not improve selection. LP (natively per-sample) provides distinct scores but is too compressed to discriminate (\S\ref{sec:discussion}).}
18	\label{fig:overview}
19	\end{figure*}
20	
21	We evaluate five signals from three families---structural, surface, and model-internal---across three NER benchmarks (SciERC \cite{luan2018scierc}, CoNLL-2003 \cite{tjongkimsang2003conll}, WNUT-17 \cite{derczynski2017wnut}), using two 8B models (Qwen3-8B, LLaMA-3.1-8B) with a 4B ablation (Qwen3-4B).
22	We include one RE benchmark (SciERC) as an exploratory extension to probe whether the gap generalizes beyond NER; RE conclusions are preliminary.
23	Our contributions:
24	\begin{enumerate}
25	    \item \textbf{The correlation-selection gap.}
26	    $\rho$ up to 0.45 coexists with selection $\Delta{\approx}0$ against 13--18~pp oracle headroom. Log-probability---the only per-sample signal---is too compressed within instances to discriminate (median range 0.041 nats, pooled within-instance $\rho(\text{LP}, \text{F1}){=}0.248$); instance-level signals, even when converted to per-sample consensus-proximity rankings (MBR), do not improve selection because utility correlates with F1 across instances but not within them. Single-metric evaluation risks selecting the wrong signal.
27	
28	    \item \textbf{Diagnostic ablations.}
29	    A 4-seed free-form decoding ablation confirms NER compression is not caused by constrained decoding; entity-token LP increases within-instance variance ($4{\times}$) but worsens selection, pointing to intrinsic model overconfidence on entity tokens. Verbalized confidence is even more compressed, confirming compression is not LP-specific (Appendix~\ref{app:verbalized_confidence}).
30	
31	    \item \textbf{Difficulty- and task-dependent signal rankings.}
32	    Rankings shift with task complexity and collapse at the capability boundary.\footnote{RE conclusions rest on SciERC alone (single benchmark, 40\% zero-F1 rate); we mark RE results with $^\dagger$RE throughout.}
33	\end{enumerate}
34	
35	
```

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
5	\label{sec:setup}
6	
7	\paragraph{Datasets.}
8	We evaluate on three English NER benchmarks spanning a difficulty gradient.
9	\textbf{SciERC} \cite{luan2018scierc} contains 551 test instances annotated for joint NER (6 types) and RE (7 types).
10	\textbf{CoNLL-2003} \cite{tjongkimsang2003conll} provides 3{,}453 test sentences with 4 entity types, where models achieve ${\approx}$91\% micro F1.
11	\textbf{WNUT-17} \cite{derczynski2017wnut} consists of 1{,}287 social media instances with emerging entities, used to probe method boundaries (52\% zero F1).
12	
13	\paragraph{Models and sampling.}
14	Both \textbf{Qwen3-8B} and \textbf{LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct} are fine-tuned with LoRA (rank$=$32, $\alpha{=}64$) for 5 epochs using LLaMA Factory \cite{zheng2024llamafactory} under identical training procedures.
15	Qwen3-8B's SciERC checkpoint has a minor train--test mismatch (empty thinking tokens); a quantitative analysis rules out this confound (Appendix~\ref{app:thinking_token}).
16	We draw $N{=}8$ samples at $T{=}1.0$ (primary), with $N{=}16$ for scaling experiments.\footnote{$N{=}16$ with 3 seeds verifies scaling and ranking stability ($\sigma{\leq}0.026$; Table~\ref{tab:multiseed}); gains beyond $N{=}8$ are sublinear (Appendix~\ref{app:n_scaling}).}
17	Inference uses vLLM with constrained decoding \cite{willard2023guided}.
18	
19	%% ============================================================
20	\subsection{Quality Estimation Results}
21	\label{sec:qe_results}
22	
23	\begin{table*}[t]
24	\centering
25	\small
26	\caption{Quality estimation ($\rho$ and AUROC; $N{=}8$, seed${=}42$, single-seed; 3-seed stability in Table~\ref{tab:multiseed}). Best in \textbf{bold}. $^\dagger$RE results rest on a single benchmark (SciERC, 40\% zero-F1 rate). $N{=}16$ in Table~\ref{tab:robustness}.}
27	\label{tab:quality_estimation}
28	\begin{tabular}{ll cc cc cc cc}
29	\toprule
30	& & \multicolumn{2}{c}{\textbf{SciERC NER}} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{\textbf{SciERC RE}} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{\textbf{CoNLL (Qwen)}} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{\textbf{CoNLL (LLaMA)}} \\
31	\cmidrule(lr){3-4} \cmidrule(lr){5-6} \cmidrule(lr){7-8} \cmidrule(lr){9-10}
32	Signal & Family & $\rho$ & AUC & $\rho$ & AUC & $\rho$ & AUC & $\rho$ & AUC \\
33	\midrule
34	VC & Structural & \textbf{.379} & \textbf{.663} & \textbf{.350} & .694 & .424 & .789 & .426 & .709 \\
35	SJ  & Structural & .360 & .650 & .250 & .670 & .436 & .801 & \textbf{.431} & .704 \\
36	FK & Surface & .267 & .602 & .275 & .609 & .400 & .774 & .428 & .703 \\
37	EM  & Surface    & .295 & .614 & .134 & \textbf{.706} & \textbf{.451} & \textbf{.816} & \textbf{.431} & .705 \\
38	\midrule
39	LP     & Model      & .205 & .578 & .266 & .601 & .316 & .753 & .307 & \textbf{.761} \\
40	\bottomrule
41	\end{tabular}
42	\end{table*}
43	
44	Table~\ref{tab:quality_estimation} presents $\rho$ and AUROC for five signals across tasks and models.\footnote{$\varepsilon{=}0.05$ applies only to selection (\S\ref{sec:selection_gap}); quality estimation is $\varepsilon$-free. Selection F1 is stable across $\varepsilon \in [0, 0.2]$ (Appendix~\ref{app:epsilon_robustness}).}
45	
46	\paragraph{Signal rankings are difficulty-dependent.}
47	On SciERC NER, structural signals lead (VC $\rho{=}0.379$, SJ $0.360$) over surface ($\rho{\leq}0.295$) and LP ($0.205$); bootstrap CIs confirm the SJ--LP gap ($\Delta\rho{=}{+}0.155$; Appendix~\ref{app:bootstrap_signal}).
48	On CoNLL (single-sample F1${\approx}$0.91), EM leads both $\rho$ and AUROC ($0.816$); the $\rho$--AUROC dissociation across datasets reflects use-case alignment.
49	On $^\dagger$RE, LP rises above SJ and FK at $N{=}16$ (Table~\ref{tab:multiseed}).
50	At the capability boundary---WNUT-17 (52\% zero F1)---all conditional signals collapse (Table~\ref{tab:wnut}); restricting to non-zero instances reshuffles rankings, with EM strongest on NER ($\rho{=}0.360$) and $^\dagger$RE ($\rho{=}0.417$) while LP $^\dagger$RE collapses to $\rho{=}0.011$ (Figure~\ref{fig:signal_heatmap}, Appendix~\ref{app:additional}).
51	
52	%% ============================================================
53	\subsection{The Correlation-Selection Gap}
54	\label{sec:selection_gap}
55	
56	We define the correlation-selection gap as the disparity between a signal's instance-level rank correlation with F1 and its ability to select the best output from $N$ candidates.
57	
58	\begin{table}[t]
59	\centering
60	\small

58	\begin{table}[t]
59	\centering
60	\small
61	\caption{Best-of-$N$ selection F1 on SciERC ($N{=}8$, $T{=}1.0$). LP is per-sample; others are instance-level. NER: 2-seed mean${\pm}\sigma$, Fisher $p$ vs.\ single-sample; $^\dagger$RE: single seed. Best non-oracle in \textbf{bold}. $^\ddagger$Single: one $T{=}1.0$ draw.}
62	\label{tab:selection}
63	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
64	\toprule
65	Method & NER F1 & $p$ & RE F1 \\
66	\midrule
67	Oracle & $0.778 \pm 0.003$ & --- & 0.585 \\
68	\midrule
69	\multicolumn{4}{l}{\textit{Per-sample signal}} \\
70	LP           & $\mathbf{0.647} \pm 0.005$ & .29 & 0.395 \\
71	\midrule
72	\multicolumn{4}{l}{\textit{Instance-level signals (consensus-proximity selection)}} \\
73	SJ           & $0.646 \pm 0.002$ & .68 & 0.386 \\
74	FK           & $0.646 \pm 0.001$ & .79 & --- \\
75	EM           & $0.637 \pm 0.001$ & .45 & 0.372 \\
76	VC           & $0.623 \pm 0.004$ & .26 & 0.359 \\
77	\midrule
78	Single$^\ddagger$ & $0.645 \pm 0.001$ & --- & \textbf{0.404} \\
79	Random           & $0.610 \pm 0.003$ & --- & 0.354 \\
80	\bottomrule
81	\end{tabular}
82	\end{table}
83	
84	Despite moderate-to-strong quality estimation, confidence signals yield limited selection gains (Table~\ref{tab:selection}, Figure~\ref{fig:selection_gap}).
85	Instance-level signals (SJ, FK, EM, VC) assign identical QE scores to all candidates per input; consensus-proximity (MBR) converts these to per-sample rankings (Table~\ref{tab:selection}: SJ 0.646, FK 0.646, EM 0.637, VC 0.623), but none improves over single-sample.
86	The key question is whether LP---the only natively per-sample signal---can exploit the oracle headroom.
87	
88	\paragraph{No significant improvement over single-sample.}
89	On SciERC NER (2-seed sign tests\footnote{NER uses 2 seeds to estimate variance; $^\dagger$RE uses a single seed because it serves as an exploratory extension on a single benchmark. Multi-seed NER experiments confirm seed variation is small ($\sigma{\leq}0.026$; Table~\ref{tab:multiseed}).}, $n{=}529$), the oracle (0.778) reveals 13.3~pp of headroom above single-sample (0.645), yet LP achieves only 0.647 ($\Delta{=}{+}0.2$~pp, $p{=}0.29$); no signal consistently improves selection ($p{>}0.05$; TOST equivalence holds under all external anchors for LLaMA-CoNLL but varies by configuration, Appendix~\ref{app:tost}).
90	On $^\dagger$RE, the best signal (LP, 0.395) falls below single-sample (0.404), with 18.1~pp unrealized; excluding zero-F1 instances worsens the gap (VC $-10.0$~pp; Appendix~\ref{app:re_conditional_selection}).
91	The pattern holds across CoNLL and WNUT-17 (Table~\ref{tab:cross_selection}).
92	CoNLL's near-ceiling single-sample F1 (0.91) with only ${\sim}$4~pp oracle headroom functions primarily as a ceiling-effect control.
93	Under stringent external TOST anchors ($\delta_0{=}1.0$~pp), some configurations (e.g., Qwen CoNLL) show statistically significant degradation, indicating signals can actively harm selection (Appendix~\ref{app:tost}).
94	Instance-level signals predict \textit{which inputs} the model handles reliably, not \textit{which output} is best; closing this gap likely requires trained verifiers \cite{cobbe2021verifiers}.\footnote{For negative results (all $p{>}0.05$), uncorrected tests suffice; the sole positive finding (non-tied LP subset, $+2.9$~pp, $p{=}0.004$; \S\ref{sec:discussion}) survives Bonferroni correction over 5 comparisons.}
95	
96	%% ============================================================
97	\subsection{Robustness}
98	\label{sec:robustness_main}
99	
100	Consistency signals are robust across $T{\in}\{0.7, 1.0\}$ with sublinear $N$-scaling gains (Table~\ref{tab:robustness}); LP shows the largest temperature sensitivity ($\Delta\rho{=}{+}0.044$).
101	NER calibration is reasonable (ECE 0.069--0.096); $^\dagger$RE miscalibration (ECE${\leq}$0.339) caps $\rho$ at ${\approx}0.28$ for $N{=}16$ (Appendix~\ref{app:calibration}).
102	
103	\begin{table}[t]
104	\centering
105	\small
106	\caption{Scale ablation: Qwen3-8B vs.\ 4B quality estimation ($\rho$; $N{=}8$, $T{=}1.0$, seed${=}42$). 8B values identical to Table~\ref{tab:quality_estimation}; 4B uses matched evaluation protocol. $^\dagger$Preliminary.}
107	\label{tab:scale_ablation}
108	\begin{tabular}{@{}ll cc r@{}}
109	\toprule
110	& & \textbf{8B} & \textbf{4B} & $\Delta$ \\
111	\midrule
112	CoNLL NER & SJ & .436 & .422 & $-$.014 \\
113	          & FK & .400 & .418 & $+$.018 \\
114	SciERC NER & SJ & .360 & .422 & $+$.062 \\
115	           & FK & .267 & .326 & $+$.059 \\
116	SciERC RE$^\dagger$ & SJ & .250 & .267 & $+$.017 \\
117	           & FK & .275 & .359 & $+$.084 \\
118	\bottomrule
119	\end{tabular}
120	\end{table}
121	
122	\paragraph{Scale robustness.}
123	Repeating with Qwen3-4B (Table~\ref{tab:scale_ablation}), quality estimation is comparable or improved (mean $|\Delta\rho|{=}0.042$), yet selection remains below single-sample on SciERC ($\Delta$ up to $-3.4$~pp) despite 13.0~pp of oracle headroom, and indistinguishable on CoNLL (${<}0.3$~pp, 1.7~pp headroom). Within the 3B--8B range tested across two model families, task difficulty is a stronger predictor of the gap than model scale.
124

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01TvCDoGhKyB7uNVHXdvgyrX`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Practical recommendations.}
4	VC leads on hard NER ($\rho{=}0.379$); EM leads easy NER and binary discrimination (AUROC${=}0.816$, CoNLL); on $^\dagger$RE, LP outperforms structural signals (Table~\ref{tab:multiseed}).
5	Rankings hold across model families and a 4B ablation (Table~\ref{tab:scale_ablation}); at the capability boundary (WNUT-17), all signals collapse.
6	
7	\paragraph{Why the gap exists.}
8	Four of five signals are instance-level aggregates that assign a single QE score per input. Consensus-proximity (MBR) converts these to per-sample rankings (Table~\ref{tab:selection}), instantiating MBR decoding \cite{eikema2022sampling} with signal-specific utility functions. Despite MBR's success in machine translation \cite{freitag2022high}, all variants fail here because utility correlates with F1 across instances but not within a single instance---the fundamental asymmetry underlying the gap (Appendix~\ref{app:mbr_discussion}).
9	Log-probability, the only per-sample signal, is severely compressed: median LP range is 0.041 nats (IQR [0.024, 0.073]; Table~\ref{tab:logprob_ties}), with pooled within-instance $\rho(\text{LP}, \text{F1}){=}0.248$ confirming weak but positive signal.
10	Where compression is mitigated ($n{=}210$ instances with above-median spread), LP selection outperforms single-sample ($+2.9$~pp, $p{=}0.004$), confirming compression as the bottleneck; the gap holds even among high-diversity instances (Appendix~\ref{app:diversity}).
11	Stratifying by voting confidence reveals a complementary mechanism: high-consistency instances (Q4) have collapsed headroom (4.78~pp), leaving nothing to select, while low-consistency instances retain headroom but unreliable LP rankings (Appendix~\ref{app:vc_quartile}).
12	Doubling to $N{=}16$ expands oracle headroom by $+4.4$~pp without improving selection; on $^\dagger$RE at 4B scale, LP is the sole positive signal ($\Delta{=}{+}0.7$~pp, $p{=}0.058$; Table~\ref{tab:re_n16_selection}), consistent with LP being the strongest RE selection signal across scales, though still below single-sample at 8B.
13	A verbalized confidence baseline confirms that score compression is not LP-specific: the model's self-rated confidence (mean 8.6/10) is even more compressed and yields the weakest selection despite higher $\rho$ than LP (Appendix~\ref{app:verbalized_confidence}; \citealp{bhattacharyya2026beyond}).
14	
15	\paragraph{Hypotheses for LP compression.}
16	We identify four (non-mutually-exclusive) mechanisms that may explain within-instance LP compression, though we do not isolate their individual contributions:
17	(a) \textit{token-level averaging}---mean log-probability over long sequences smooths token-level variance, compressing the effective signal;
18	(b) \textit{schema-dominated tokens}---constrained decoding enforces structural tokens (brackets, type labels) that carry no extraction information yet dominate the sequence, reducing effective variance; and
19	(c) \textit{vocabulary restriction}---constrained decoding shrinks the effective vocabulary at each step, concentrating probability mass and reducing log-probability variation; and
20	(d) \textit{format-token dilution}---fine-tuned models assign extremely high LP to schema tokens (\texttt{\{"entities": [}~etc.) that carry no extraction information, diluting the signal from entity-name tokens; free-form parse rates of 99.95\% confirm that format tokens dominate the sequence.
21	Mechanisms (b)--(c) predict that free-form decoding should reduce compression; our NER ablation finds negligible change (std ratio 0.97; Appendix~\ref{app:freeform_ablation}), suggesting (a) and (d) are the primary drivers for NER.
22	
23	\paragraph{Ablation: free-form decoding.}\label{sec:ablation}
24	Removing grammar constraints (SciERC NER, Qwen3-8B, 4 seeds) yields nearly identical LP distributions (std ratio 0.97) and negligible selection changes (Appendix~\ref{app:freeform_ablation}), ruling out constrained decoding as the cause of NER compression.
25	On $^\dagger$RE, LP selection consistently reverses direction under free-form decoding ($+1.8{\pm}0.1$~pp, 4 seeds; Appendix~\ref{app:freeform_ablation}), while under constrained decoding LP falls below single-sample ($-0.9$~pp at 8B).
26	RE LP selection is thus regime-dependent: constrained decoding suppresses it, free-form decoding recovers it, and at 4B $N{=}16$ it is marginally positive ($+0.7$~pp, $p{=}0.058$).
27	This consistent directional reversal across 4 seeds is a promising signal: free-form decoding permits greater structural diversity in RE outputs, where LP variation---though compressed---aligns with extraction quality in ways that constrained decoding's fixed template obscures.
28	VC becomes per-sample in the selection context (each candidate's overlap with the majority-voted output varies), yet tends to select the most typical rather than the best output---consistent with MBR's known mode-seeking bias \cite{eikema2022sampling, deguchi2024centroid}.
29	More broadly, all consensus-proximity strategies fail because utility correlates with F1 across instances but not within them (Appendix~\ref{app:mbr_discussion}).
30	
31	\paragraph{Ablation: entity-token LP.}
32	We directly test hypothesis (d) by isolating entity-mention tokens from the log-probability computation.
33	On SciERC, entity-token LP exhibits $4{\times}$ higher within-instance variance than full-sequence LP, confirming that format tokens do compress the signal.
34	However, entity-token LP selection performs \textit{worse} than full-sequence LP ($-2.0$~pp), and on CoNLL, entity-token LP variance collapses to near-zero (median $2{\times}10^{-10}$) with 79\% of instances tied.
35	This rules out format-token dilution as the primary cause of LP's selection failure and instead points to an intrinsic pattern: fine-tuned models assign near-deterministic probabilities to entity-name tokens themselves, leaving minimal discriminative signal regardless of aggregation scope.
36	
37	\paragraph{Pilot remedy.}
38	Entity-token LP represents a natural pilot remedy: if format-token dilution caused compression, restricting LP to entity tokens should recover selection ability.
39	Its failure---particularly the near-zero entity-token variance on CoNLL---suggests that effective remedies must address the underlying model confidence calibration rather than signal aggregation.
40	
41	\paragraph{Methodological implications.}
42	The decoupling of $\rho$, AUROC, and selection F1 means single-metric evaluation risks selecting the wrong signal.
43	Effective selection likely requires trained verifiers \cite{cobbe2021verifiers, venktesh2025trust}; $N{=}8$ captures most quality estimation benefit (Appendix~\ref{app:n_scaling}).
44	Structural signals are robust to domain shift when the training schema subsumes the target (Appendix~\ref{app:ood}).
45	
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is very revealing. Let me now check:

1. The "Single" baseline in Table 2 — is this greedy (T=0) or a random T=1 sample?
2. Whether MBR within-instance utility-F1 data exists in appendix
3. CoNLL greedy/selection data

Key observations so far:
- Table 2 shows "Single†" = 0.645 for NER, 0.404 for RE — footnoted as "one T=1.0 draw"
- So "Single" is NOT T=0 greedy — it's a random T=1 sample


- The code actually does compute T=0 greedy and stores it as `greedy_f1` in the report (SciERC NER reaches 0.663), but the paper's selection table compares against the T=1 random sample instead — this is crucial for understanding SF4.
- Looking at the actual numbers: T=0 greedy scores 0.663, which already outperforms the paper's LP selection method at 0.647 and the single T=1 draw at 0.645, suggesting the greedy baseline is stronger than the proposed approach.
- This means the SF4 data already exists in the codebase and just needs to be surfaced in the table, which would fundamentally change how the results are presented.

Now I'm evaluating whether Few-NERD would work for MF2. The dataset has 66 fine-grained entity types compared to SciERC's 6 and CoNLL's 4, which creates a massive schema for constrained decoding. While it's a solid general-domain dataset from a reputable source, the type complexity and potential challenges with LoRA fine-tuning on that many types make it a questionable fit.

I should also look at the MBR within-instance utility-F1 metrics to see how they compare.

5	\begin{table}[h]

7	\small
8	\begin{tabular}{cl}
9	\toprule
10	Symbol & Meaning \\
11	\midrule
12	$\mathcal{M}$ & Language model \\
13	$x$ & Input instance \\
14	$y_i$ & $i$-th stochastic sample \\
15	$N$ & Number of samples \\
16	$T$ & Sampling temperature \\
17	$c(\cdot)$ & Confidence signal \\
18	SJ & Soft Jaccard similarity \\
19	FK & Fleiss' $\kappa$ \\
20	EM & Exact-match voting rate \\
21	VC ($c_{\mathrm{vc}}$) & Entity voting confidence \\
22	LP ($c_{\mathrm{lp}}$) & Log-probability (mean token log-likelihood) \\
23	\bottomrule
24	\end{tabular}
25	\caption{Notation used throughout the paper.}
26	\label{tab:notation}
27	\end{table}
28	
29	\section{Robustness Analysis}\label{app:robustness}
30	
31	We exclude instances with empty gold annotations from all correlation analyses (CoNLL: 697/3{,}453 instances, 20.2\%; SciERC: 22/551, 4.0\%).
32	For these instances, F1 is trivially zero regardless of prediction quality, and consistency signals reflect agreement on the empty set rather than extraction quality---including them inflates or deflates Spearman $\rho$ depending on signal behavior at the boundary.
33	
34	\begin{table}[t]
35	\centering
36	\small
37	\caption{Signal robustness across configurations (Spearman $\rho$). Top: temperature on Qwen SciERC. Middle: sample size on Qwen SciERC. Bottom: transfer across model families (both 8B) (LLaMA CoNLL $N{=}16$, 3-seed mean $\pm\sigma$). $^\dagger$Mean over 3 seeds ($\sigma{=}0.013$; individual: 0.398, 0.423, 0.410). $\sigma$ computed as sample standard deviation.}
38	\label{tab:robustness}
39	\begin{tabular}{llcc}
40	\toprule

```text
93-
94-\paragraph{FK independence: instance-level permutation test.}\label{app:fk_independence}
95-
96:To verify that Fleiss' $\kappa$ reflects genuine within-instance agreement rather than marginal-rate artifacts, we conduct instance-level permutation tests across three configurations.
97-For each instance, we permute sample labels 10{,}000 times to generate a null distribution of FK under independence, then compare the observed FK against this null via a one-sample $t$-test and permutation $p$-value.
98-
99-\begin{table}[t]
100-\centering
101-\small
102:\caption{Instance-level FK independence test. Permutation tests confirm FK reflects true within-instance agreement (all $p{<}0.001$). Split-half reliability $r$ computed by randomly partitioning $N$ samples into two halves and correlating their FK scores.}
103-\label{tab:fk_independence}
104-\begin{tabular}{lrcccc}
105-\toprule
--
481-
482-\paragraph{LP shift magnitude.}
483-Empty thinking tokens (mean 4.5 per sample) constitute 2.1\% of tokens on SciERC.
484:The estimated LP shift is 0.001 nats per token (2.68\% of the median within-instance LP range of 0.041 nats).
485-Because all samples share identical thinking-token LP offsets, cumulative LP rankings are invariant; mean LP changes selection for ${<}1.5\%$ of instances.
486-
487-\paragraph{Constant prefix cancellation.}
488-All $N$ samples within an instance share the same prompt and, under constrained decoding, the same absence of thinking tokens at inference time.
489:Any LP bias from the training-time thinking prefix is therefore a near-constant additive shift across all candidates within an instance, leaving within-instance LP \textit{rankings} unchanged.
490-
491-\paragraph{CoNLL control group.}
492-CoNLL and WNUT-17 checkpoints were trained without thinking tokens (\texttt{enable\_thinking=False}).
--
495-
496-\section{Log-Probability Score Compression}\label{app:logprob_ties}
497-
498:Under constrained decoding, log-probability scores are severely compressed within instances, limiting per-sample discrimination (Table~\ref{tab:logprob_ties}, Figure~\ref{fig:lp_range_cdf}).
499:The median within-instance LP range is only 0.041 nats; we use $\varepsilon{=}0.05$ as an illustrative threshold to characterize this compression.
500-At this cutpoint, 60.3\% of instances ($n{=}529$, excluding empty gold annotations) have a within-sample range below $\varepsilon$, and 75\% of pairwise comparisons are effective ties ($|\Delta|{<}0.026$).
501-The CDF rises smoothly with no cliff or discontinuity (Figure~\ref{fig:lp_range_cdf}), confirming that compression is a pervasive regime rather than a bimodal split.
502-This compression is stable across seeds: seed${=}456$ yields median LP range${=}0.040$ nats and tied fraction${=}62.2\%$ (vs.\ seed${=}42$: $0.041$ nats, $60.3\%$).
--
506-\begin{figure}[t]
507-\centering
508-\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_lp_range_cdf}
509:\caption{CDF of within-instance LP range on SciERC NER ($N{=}8$, $n{=}529$). The median LP range is 0.041 nats (dashed line), below $\varepsilon{=}0.05$. The smooth CDF confirms compression is pervasive, not bimodal.}
510-\label{fig:lp_range_cdf}
511-\end{figure}
512-
513-\begin{table}[t]
514-\centering
515-\small
516:\caption{Log-probability score compression on SciERC NER ($N{=}8$, $n{=}529$). ``Tied'' = fraction of instances where within-instance LP range ${<}\varepsilon$.}
517-\label{tab:logprob_ties}
518-\begin{tabular}{lc}
519-\toprule
--
528-\end{table}
529-
530-\paragraph{Non-tied subset.}
531:Among instances with LP range ${\geq}0.05$ ($n{=}210$, 40\% of total), log-probability selection \textit{significantly} outperforms single-sample ($+2.9$~pp F1, $p{=}0.004$), and within-instance $\rho(\text{LP}, \text{F1})$ rises from 0.08 (tied subset) to 0.39 (non-tied subset).
532:As a continuous complement to the binary tied/non-tied analysis, pooled within-instance $\rho(\text{LP}, \text{F1}){=}0.248$ across all instances confirms weak but positive per-sample signal; however, only 21.6\% of instances achieve $p{<}0.05$ significance, and $\rho(\text{LP range}, \text{F1 range}){=}0.328$ shows that instances with more LP spread also exhibit more F1 variability---LP carries information but compression limits its exploitation.
533-A free-form NER decoding ablation (Appendix~\ref{app:freeform_ablation}) confirms this compression persists without constrained decoding.
534-
535-\paragraph{$\varepsilon$ sensitivity sweep (post-hoc exploratory).}
--
540-\begin{table}[t]
541-\centering
542-\small
543:\caption{$\varepsilon$ sensitivity sweep for LP selection on SciERC NER ($N{=}8$, seed${=}42$, $n{=}529$). ``Tied\%'' = fraction with within-instance LP range ${<}\varepsilon$. $\Delta$: selection F1 gain over single-sample on the non-tied subset. The tied fraction increases smoothly; $\varepsilon{=}0.05$ yields the strongest statistical separation.}
544-\label{tab:epsilon_sweep}
545-\begin{tabular}{ccccc}
546-\toprule
--
679-\end{tabular}
680-\end{table}
681-
682:\section{MBR Decoding and Task-Specific Interactions}\label{app:mbr_discussion}
683-
684-\paragraph{Connection to MBR decoding.}
685-Our selection strategies instantiate minimum Bayes risk (MBR) decoding \cite{eikema2022sampling, muller2021understanding}: each selects $\hat{y} = \arg\max_{y_i} \sum_{j \neq i} U(y_i, y_j)$ with utility $U$ defined by SJ, EM, or entity overlap.
686:Despite MBR's success in machine translation \cite{freitag2022high} and its extension to summarization, code generation, and captioning \cite{jinnai2024mbr}, all variants fail here because the utility functions correlate with F1 at the instance level but not within a single instance---the mathematical essence of the correlation-selection gap.
687-\citet{eikema2025structured} study structure-conditional MBR decoding and find that standard utility functions degrade on structured outputs---consistent with our finding that consensus-proximity selection fails in IE despite its success in machine translation.
688-
689-\paragraph{Task-specific decoding interactions.}
--
692-The win/loss ratio shifts from 36:59 (constrained) to 45:45 (free-form), with the gains concentrated on relation-dense instances (mean 3.3 gold relations).
693-This interaction does not generalize to NER, where selection remains negligible under both regimes.
694-This ablation uses a single seed (seed${=}42$); whether the RE reversal replicates across seeds and generalizes to other RE benchmarks warrants further investigation.
695:The non-tied subset ($n{=}210$) is partially reshuffled under free-form decoding: Jaccard similarity between the constrained and free-form non-tied sets is 0.472, indicating that 87 instances newly gain LP discrimination while others lose it, yielding a $3{\times}$ increase in within-instance LP range for the newly non-tied subset.
696-Despite this reshuffling, aggregate NER selection gains remain negligible---score compression is not a fixed property of specific instances but rather a pervasive regime that shifts across instances without producing net selection improvement.
697-Free-form decoding also slightly improves quality estimation (NER VC $\rho$: 0.379$\to$0.409), though this does not translate to selection gains.
698-
--
700-
701-Table~\ref{tab:freeform_ablation} compares constrained and free-form decoding across four dimensions on SciERC ($N{=}8$, $T{=}1.0$, Qwen3-8B; seed${=}42$ shown, 4-seed RE statistics below).
702-Free-form decoding removes grammar constraints entirely; outputs are parsed post-hoc (parse rate 99.95\%, 4406/4408).
703:LP score distributions are nearly identical across regimes (Panel~A: within-instance std ratio $0.97{\times}$), and NER selection gains remain negligible under both regimes (Panel~B).
704-RE LP selection consistently reverses direction under free-form decoding across 4 seeds: $+1.84{\pm}0.12$~pp (seeds 42/123/456/789: $+1.69$, $+1.76$, $+2.00$, $+1.91$~pp) vs.\ $-0.68$~pp constrained.
705-The constrained reference ($-0.68$~pp) uses the ablation-matched baseline; the primary experiment (Table~\ref{tab:selection}) yields $-0.9$~pp on the same gold-filtered set.
706-Quality estimation improves modestly for most signals under free-form decoding (Panel~C), with the largest gain for FK ($+0.053$ NER, $+0.039$ RE).
```

```text
Now I have all the key data. Let me compile my findings:

## Key Data Points

### Greedy (T=0) baseline already exists:
- SciERC NER: greedy_f1 = 0.6633 (T=0 argmax)
- CoNLL NER: greedy_f1 = 0.9067 (T=0 argmax)

### Paper Table 2 (Selection):
- SciERC NER: Oracle 0.778, LP 0.647, SJ 0.646, Single† 0.645, Random 0.610


- The "Single†" entry is explicitly one T=1.0 draw, not the T=0 greedy baseline

### Critical finding for SF4:
The T=0 greedy approach at 0.663 outperforms all the N=8 T=1 selection methods—LP at 0.647, SJ at 0.646, and Single at 0.645—by a significant margin of 1.6 percentage points. This is striking because it means spending 8× the compute on sampling and selection still can't beat the free argmax, and this comparison is already available in the existing pipeline outputs without needing new experiments.

### MBR within-instance utility-F1 gap:
Appendix L discusses MBR but doesn't provide the specific within-instance utility-F1 correlation coefficient. Line 686 mentions that utility functions correlate with F1 at the instance level but not within single instances, but this claim isn't quantified with an actual ρ value. The data exists in samples.jsonl with all pairwise utilities—it just needs the analysis code to compute it.

### Few-NERD considerations:
Few-NERD is a well-established large-scale NER dataset with 66 fine-grained types across 8 coarse categories, but the sheer number of types makes the constrained decoding schema unwieldy.  The INTRA split offers medium difficulty by keeping examples within the same coarse type, though I'm leaning toward using the 8 coarse types rather than all 66 fine-grained ones for practicality. OntoNotes 5.0 and MultiNERD are alternatives, but Few-NERD coarse seems like the better fit.

### MF1 framing issue:
The current abstract and intro text lead with the definitional aspect of MBR consensus-proximity and utility correlation, which is technically accurate but doesn't capture the core finding as effectively. I need to reframe this to emphasize the actual result rather than the mechanism.

### MF2 assessment:
The 8-hour estimate for Few-NERD feels optimistic given the full pipeline of data preparation, fine-tuning, debugging, and analysis. More critically, I need to see whether Few-NERD actually demonstrates the problem I'm trying to highlight—if it doesn't show the same pattern, the whole benchmark choice becomes questionable.

Actually, there's a deeper issue here. If Few-NERD (general domain, medium difficulty) shows no gap while SciERC does, reviewers will argue the phenomenon is specific to scientific text rather than a fundamental issue with the approach. But the gap already manifests across SciERC and CoNLL, so adding a third dataset that contradicts this pattern would actually undermine the thesis rather than strengthen it as a boundary condition.

The real value of Few-NERD would be confirming the gap holds in general domain too, but the time cost is substantial—the dataset is much larger than SciERC with 340K training examples, so fine-tuning would take significantly longer than the initial estimates suggest. The supervised split with 66K training examples is still substantially larger than what's feasible in the timeframe, so Few-NERD is looking like a significant risk. I'm considering whether I could instead evaluate an existing fine-tuned model on a different NER dataset without additional fine-tuning, but that doesn't align with what the paper actually does. A better path forward might be reframing this as medium-difficulty NER rather than few-shot, which is more honest about the scope and lets me position it along a difficulty gradient.

For SF2, I notice the VC quartile analysis is already computed in the existing data, so instead of the ad-hoc threshold approach, I can stratify the LP selection performance by quartile to see where the strategy actually helps.

For SF4, the greedy baseline at T=0 is already present in the results, which is a critical finding I should highlight.

Looking at the numbers: SciERC NER shows greedy at 0.663 outperforming the LP selection approach at 0.647, and CoNLL NER has greedy at 0.907 versus selection around 0.899. The paper's "Single†" baseline is explicitly a single T=1.0 sample, not the greedy T=0 approach. This means I don't need a new experiment—the data already exists in the tables. Adding a "Greedy (T=0)" row to Table 2 would make the comparison stark: eight times the compute and sophisticated selection methods can't even match the simple argmax. Let me verify that the greedy implementation in the code is actually using temperature zero. The paper's oracle headroom is measured against the single T=1 sample baseline (13.3pp), but the code computes it against T=0 greedy instead (11.5pp). If I add greedy to the results table, it shows T=0 greedy actually outperforms the single T=1 sample, which means LP selection barely improves over random sampling in this setup.

This is a striking finding for the director's question about whether the experiment is worth doing—the data already exists in the paper, just not presented this way. Let me check the same pattern across CoNLL and SciERC RE to see if greedy consistently outperforms the selection methods.

Looking at the SciERC RE results, greedy at 0.429 beats single-sample T=1 at 0.404 and LP selection at 0.395, which is a 3.4pp advantage. This confirms the pattern: T=0 greedy dominates all selection approaches across datasets. The reason is that fine-tuned models at T=0 produce the mode of the distribution, which aligns with gold standards, whereas T=1 sampling pulls from the full distribution including worse outputs. This actually suggests the paper's headroom calculation might be overstated—computing it as oracle minus single T=1 makes the gap look larger than it really is.

The real issue is that selection performance sits below the T=0 greedy baseline itself, not just below the oracle. For NER, the oracle is 13.3pp above single T=1, but only 11.5pp above T=0 greedy, and selection at 0.647 falls short of T=0 greedy at 0.663. So the problem isn't filling the headroom—it's that selection can't even match the baseline.

Now I'm thinking through the strategic implications for the review. R1 is borderline at 3.5 while R2 is at 3.0, so the path forward depends on whether the authors can make substantial improvements in the next eight days. The critical bottleneck is whether MF1 works on the eleventh attempt—if it fails again, that's wasted time.

Looking at what's actually feasible: MF1 needs Q1 data first before reframing, MF2 requires narrowing the claim and avoiding the Few-NERD risk, SF4 already has the greedy baseline data available, and SF2 can leverage existing VC quartile data for cheap additional analysis. Moving RE to the appendix is the right call. The Findings track looks achievable, but Main Conference is a real stretch given the timeline and remaining uncertainties.

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_014J2qUdQBnVN7pdmpReGFUm`</summary>

```text
94-\paragraph{FK independence: instance-level permutation test.}\label{app:fk_independence}
95-
96:To verify that Fleiss' $\kappa$ reflects genuine within-instance agreement rather than marginal-rate artifacts, we conduct instance-level permutation tests across three configurations.
97-For each instance, we permute sample labels 10{,}000 times to generate a null distribution of FK under independence, then compare the observed FK against this null via a one-sample $t$-test and permutation $p$-value.
98-
--
167-Consensus-proximity selection chooses $\hat{y} = \arg\max_{y_i} \sum_{j \neq i} \text{SJ}(y_i, y_j)$.
168-Leave-one-out SJ (LOO-SJ) for sample $y_i$ is $\text{LOO-SJ}_i = \frac{1}{N-1}\sum_{j \neq i} \text{SJ}(y_i, y_j)$.
169:Since the $\frac{1}{N-1}$ constant is shared across all candidates within an instance, $\arg\max_{y_i} \text{LOO-SJ}_i = \arg\max_{y_i} \sum_{j \neq i} \text{SJ}(y_i, y_j)$, making the two selection rules equivalent.
170-Standard (non-LOO) SJ adds the self-comparison term $\text{SJ}(y_i, y_i) = 1$ to each candidate, which is constant and thus does not affect ranking.
171-Empirically, LOO-SJ yields $\Delta\rho{=}{-}0.014$ relative to standard SJ (a correlation-level difference), with a maximum per-instance score difference of $3.1{\times}10^{-4}$ (negligible at the individual prediction level).
--
487-\paragraph{Constant prefix cancellation.}
488-All $N$ samples within an instance share the same prompt and, under constrained decoding, the same absence of thinking tokens at inference time.
489:Any LP bias from the training-time thinking prefix is therefore a near-constant additive shift across all candidates within an instance, leaving within-instance LP \textit{rankings} unchanged.
490-
491-\paragraph{CoNLL control group.}
--
530-\paragraph{Non-tied subset.}
531-Among instances with LP range ${\geq}0.05$ ($n{=}210$, 40\% of total), log-probability selection \textit{significantly} outperforms single-sample ($+2.9$~pp F1, $p{=}0.004$), and within-instance $\rho(\text{LP}, \text{F1})$ rises from 0.08 (tied subset) to 0.39 (non-tied subset).
532:As a continuous complement to the binary tied/non-tied analysis, pooled within-instance $\rho(\text{LP}, \text{F1}){=}0.248$ across all instances confirms weak but positive per-sample signal; however, only 21.6\% of instances achieve $p{<}0.05$ significance, and $\rho(\text{LP range}, \text{F1 range}){=}0.328$ shows that instances with more LP spread also exhibit more F1 variability---LP carries information but compression limits its exploitation.
533-A free-form NER decoding ablation (Appendix~\ref{app:freeform_ablation}) confirms this compression persists without constrained decoding.
534-
--
684-\paragraph{Connection to MBR decoding.}
685-Our selection strategies instantiate minimum Bayes risk (MBR) decoding \cite{eikema2022sampling, muller2021understanding}: each selects $\hat{y} = \arg\max_{y_i} \sum_{j \neq i} U(y_i, y_j)$ with utility $U$ defined by SJ, EM, or entity overlap.
686:Despite MBR's success in machine translation \cite{freitag2022high} and its extension to summarization, code generation, and captioning \cite{jinnai2024mbr}, all variants fail here because the utility functions correlate with F1 at the instance level but not within a single instance---the mathematical essence of the correlation-selection gap.
687-\citet{eikema2025structured} study structure-conditional MBR decoding and find that standard utility functions degrade on structured outputs---consistent with our finding that consensus-proximity selection fails in IE despite its success in machine translation.
688-
--
701-Table~\ref{tab:freeform_ablation} compares constrained and free-form decoding across four dimensions on SciERC ($N{=}8$, $T{=}1.0$, Qwen3-8B; seed${=}42$ shown, 4-seed RE statistics below).
702-Free-form decoding removes grammar constraints entirely; outputs are parsed post-hoc (parse rate 99.95\%, 4406/4408).
703:LP score distributions are nearly identical across regimes (Panel~A: within-instance std ratio $0.97{\times}$), and NER selection gains remain negligible under both regimes (Panel~B).
704-RE LP selection consistently reverses direction under free-form decoding across 4 seeds: $+1.84{\pm}0.12$~pp (seeds 42/123/456/789: $+1.69$, $+1.76$, $+2.00$, $+1.91$~pp) vs.\ $-0.68$~pp constrained.
705-The constrained reference ($-0.68$~pp) uses the ablation-matched baseline; the primary experiment (Table~\ref{tab:selection}) yields $-0.9$~pp on the same gold-filtered set.
--
923-The CoNLL-trained model recognizes only 4 news entity types (PER, ORG, LOC, MISC) and has no relation extraction capability; when applied to SciERC's 6 scientific entity types and 7 relation types, the model cannot produce meaningful structured outputs (single-sample NER F1${=}0.009$, RE F1${=}0.001$).
924-Under this regime, most signals flip to negative or near-zero correlations (Table~\ref{tab:ood}); only VC retains weak positive correlation ($\rho{=}0.105$, $p{=}0.016$).
925:These negative correlations reflect the model systematically generating confident but incorrect outputs---a capability failure analogous to WNUT-17 (\S\ref{sec:qe_results}), not evidence against signal utility.
926-
927-\paragraph{Asymmetry interpretation.}

translation.\n688-\n--\n701-Table~\\ref{tab:freeform_ablation} compares constrained and free-form decoding across four dimensions on SciERC ($N{=}8$, $T{=}1.0$, Qwen3-8B; seed${=}42$ shown, 4-seed RE statistics below).\n702-Free-form decoding removes grammar constraints entirely; outputs are parsed post-hoc (parse rate 99.95\\%, 4406/4408).\n703:LP score distributions are nearly identical across regimes (Panel~A: within-instance std ratio $0.97{\\times}$), and NER selection gains remain negligible under both regimes (Panel~B).\n704-RE LP selection consistently reverses direction under free-form decoding across 4 seeds: $+1.84{\\pm}0.12$~pp (seeds 42/123/456/789: $+1.69$, $+1.76$, $+2.00$, $+1.91$~pp) vs.\\ $-0.68$~pp constrained.\n705-The constrained reference ($-0.68$~pp) uses the ablation-matched baseline; the primary experiment (Table~\\ref{tab:selection}) yields $-0.9$~pp on the same gold-filtered set.\n--\n923-The CoNLL-trained model recognizes only 4 news entity types (PER, ORG, LOC, MISC) and has no relation extraction capability; when applied to SciERC's 6 scientific entity types and 7 relation types, the model cannot produce meaningful structured outputs (single-sample NER F1${=}0.009$, RE F1${=}0.001$).\n924-Under this regime, most signals flip to negative or near-zero correlations (Table~\\ref{tab:ood}); only VC retains weak positive correlation ($\\rho{=}0.105$, $p{=}0.016$).\n925:These negative correlations reflect the model systematically generating confident but incorrect outputs---a capability failure analogous to WNUT-17
